    However, it is important to note that the individual components are not groundbreaking. The novelty lies more in the combination and application of existing techniques within the specific context of 6G edge computing for LLMs. The layer-wise similarity matching and attention-head dimensionality reduction are based on existing methods, and while the application to collaborative inference is novel, the underlying concepts are not.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments. 6G networks will require efficient and privacy-aware inference mechanisms, and CE-LSLM provides a viable solution. The potential impact includes:

    *   Enabling more sophisticated edge applications that rely on LLMs.
    *   Reducing the reliance on centralized cloud resources, improving network resilience.
    *   Protecting user privacy by processing sensitive data locally.

    The paper's significance is bolstered by the thorough experimental evaluation, which demonstrates the practical benefits of the proposed architecture. The experiments covering various workloads, deployment strategies, and request rates, although in a controlled environment, provide convincing evidence of its effectiveness. The analysis of TTFT is especially relevant for real-time applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Techniques:** Although the combination of KV cache, scheduling, layer matching, and compression adds to the paper's value, the specific compression and scheduling mechanisms used are not explored in-depth and leave room for future optimization.
    *   **Simplified Network Model:** The communication performance model, while a good starting point, could be further refined to account for more realistic network conditions, such as varying bandwidth, packet loss, and network congestion.
    *   **Controlled Environment:** The experiments are performed in a controlled lab environment, which may not fully reflect the complexities of real-world 6G deployments. Further evaluation on real 6G testbeds is needed.
    *   **Clarity and Exposition:** While technically sound, certain sections of the paper can be challenging to follow due to the heavy use of notation and technical jargon. The paper could benefit from a clearer and more intuitive presentation of the core ideas. Some arguments are not well substantiated by experiments.

* **Impact:** The paper could influence researchers and practitioners working on edge AI, LLM deployment, and 6G networks. The architecture can be used for other AI inference besides LLMs. It lays the groundwork for more sophisticated collaborative inference systems in future wireless networks.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a valuable and well-executed solution to the challenge of deploying LLMs in resource-constrained edge environments.  While the individual components are not entirely novel, the combination of techniques, the focus on 6G network constraints, and the thorough experimental evaluation make it a significant contribution.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

A score of 7 reflects the incremental novelty but notable practical value of the paper. CE-LSLM doesn't introduce revolutionary new concepts, but it effectively combines existing techniques to address a relevant and important problem in the field of edge AI. The strong experimental results demonstrate the practical benefits of the proposed architecture, and the paper's focus on 6G network constraints makes it a timely contribution. The score is limited by the relatively narrow scope of the techniques explored, the simplified network model, and the controlled experimental environment. Further research is needed to validate the architecture's effectiveness in real-world 6G deployments and to explore more advanced optimization strategies.

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Beyond Chains: Bridging Large Language Models and Knowledge Bases in Complex Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14099v1)
- **Authors**: Yihua Zhu, Qianying Liu, Akiko Aizawa, Hidetoshi Shimodaira
- **Abstract**: Knowledge Base Question Answering (KBQA) aims to answer natural language questions using structured knowledge from KBs. While LLM-only approaches offer generalization, they suffer from outdated knowledge, hallucinations, and lack of transparency. Chain-based KG-RAG methods address these issues by incorporating external KBs, but are limited to simple chain-structured questions due to the absence of planning and logical structuring. Inspired by semantic parsing methods, we propose PDRR: a four-stage framework consisting of Predict, Decompose, Retrieve, and Reason. Our method first predicts the question type and decomposes the question into structured triples. Then retrieves relevant information from KBs and guides the LLM as an agent to reason over and complete the decomposed triples. Experimental results demonstrate that PDRR consistently outperforms existing methods across various LLM backbones and achieves superior performance on both chain-structured and non-chain complex questions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Beyond Chains: Bridging Large Language Models and Knowledge Bases in Complex Question Answering":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of chain-based Knowledge Graph Retrieval-Augmented Generation (KG-RAG) methods in Knowledge Base Question Answering (KBQA). Chain-based approaches are effective for simple, linear questions but struggle with questions requiring more complex reasoning, planning, or logical structures like conjunctions. The authors propose a training-free framework called Predict-Decompose-Retrieve-Reason (PDRR). PDRR first predicts the question type (chain or parallel), then decomposes the question into structured KG triples. It retrieves relevant information from KBs and guides a Large Language Model (LLM) to reason over and complete the decomposed triples. The final step is answering the question with reasoning triples. The authors demonstrate that PDRR consistently outperforms existing methods across various LLMs and KBQA datasets (CWQ and WebQSP).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the explicit planning and decomposition module before retrieval and reasoning. While KG-RAG is a well-explored area, the explicit incorporation of a planning stage, reminiscent of semantic parsing but in a training-free manner with LLMs, is a significant contribution. The decomposition of the question into KG-style triples, guiding the LLM to execute inference step by step, is also innovative. PDRR introduces a relatively simple way to reason in different styles depending on the question.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical weakness in existing KG-RAG methods: the inability to handle complex question structures. The experiments demonstrate that PDRR consistently outperforms chain-based approaches, especially on datasets like CWQ that contain a diverse range of question types. This improvement is significant because it broadens the applicability of KG-RAG to a wider range of real-world questions. The use of LLMs without training enables broader re-use of the framework.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the limitations of existing KG-RAG methods.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** PDRR is a well-defined and modular framework. The role of each module (Predict, Decompose, Retrieve, Reason) is clear and easy to understand.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of PDRR across different LLMs and datasets. The ablation studies and the analysis by question type further strengthen the results.
    *   **Training-Free Approach:** The fact that PDRR is training-free makes it easier to implement and adapt to different KBs and domains.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Decomposition Complexity:** The reliance on LLMs for decomposition can be a weakness, as LLMs can struggle with complex logical structures. The paper acknowledges this limitation.
    *   **Limited Complexity:** The framework is only based on chain and parallel types of questions, while there are much more complex questions to be addressed.

    *   **Overhead:** Although not explicitly addressed, there is a cost to computing the type of question.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of KBQA by shifting the focus towards more explicit planning and logical structuring in KG-RAG methods. The training-free nature of PDRR could also encourage wider adoption of KG-RAG in various applications.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper provides a simple-to-implement solution for a significant problem in KBQA. While the paper notes weaknesses, the experiments are thorough and well-defined and clearly show PDRR's effectiveness.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### MultiHal: Multilingual Dataset for Knowledge-Graph Grounded Evaluation of LLM Hallucinations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14101v1)
- **Authors**: Ernests Lavrinovics, Russa Biswas, Katja Hose, Johannes Bjerva
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have inherent limitations of faithfulness and factuality, commonly referred to as hallucinations. Several benchmarks have been developed that provide a test bed for factuality evaluation within the context of English-centric datasets, while relying on supplementary informative context like web links or text passages but ignoring the available structured factual resources. To this end, Knowledge Graphs (KGs) have been identified as a useful aid for hallucination mitigation, as they provide a structured way to represent the facts about entities and their relations with minimal linguistic overhead. We bridge the lack of KG paths and multilinguality for factual language modeling within the existing hallucination evaluation benchmarks and propose a KG-based multilingual, multihop benchmark called \textbf{MultiHal} framed for generative text evaluation. As part of our data collection pipeline, we mined 140k KG-paths from open-domain KGs, from which we pruned noisy KG-paths, curating a high-quality subset of 25.9k. Our baseline evaluation shows an absolute scale increase by approximately 0.12 to 0.36 points for the semantic similarity score in KG-RAG over vanilla QA across multiple languages and multiple models, demonstrating the potential of KG integration. We anticipate MultiHal will foster future research towards several graph-based hallucination mitigation and fact-checking tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MultiHal paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MultiHal, a new multilingual benchmark dataset for evaluating the factuality and mitigating hallucinations in large language models (LLMs).  MultiHal extends existing English-centric factuality benchmarks by incorporating knowledge graph (KG) paths and providing translations in five languages (German, Spanish, French, Italian, and Portuguese). The dataset creation process involves mining KG paths from open-domain KGs, pruning noisy paths using LLM-as-a-judge, and translating questions, answers, and KG paths. The authors perform baseline experiments to demonstrate the potential of KG integration (KG-RAG) in improving semantic similarity between model predictions and ground-truth answers across multiple languages and models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the combination of multilinguality *and* KG grounding within a factuality evaluation benchmark. While there are existing datasets for factuality assessment and multilingual question answering, MultiHal addresses the specific gap of evaluating KG-grounded factuality across multiple languages. The approach of using LLM-as-a-judge for path quality filtering is relatively common, but the application within this particular dataset creation pipeline adds some value. The combination of existing datasets is a standard practice.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the dataset hinges on its ability to promote research into multilingual hallucination mitigation and fact-checking. LLM hallucinations are a major obstacle to real-world deployment. By providing a resource that facilitates cross-lingual analysis and KG integration, MultiHal can contribute to more robust and reliable LLMs, especially in low-resource languages. The baseline experiments do show a positive trend for KG-RAG.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Multilingual Focus:** Addresses a crucial gap in the factuality evaluation literature.
    *   **KG Grounding:** Leverages structured factual resources, which offers advantages over purely text-based contexts.
    *   **LLM-as-a-Judge Filtering:** Provides a scalable and potentially efficient method for curating high-quality KG paths.
    *   **Comprehensive Dataset Creation Methodology:** The paper details a well-structured pipeline, making the dataset reproducible and extensible.
    *   **Open Availability:** Publicly releasing the code and data ensures that the benchmark will be widely accessible and used.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLM-as-a-Judge:** The filtering approach depends on the reliability of LLMs, which can be biased and inconsistent. The correlation numbers are good, but not overwhelmingly high suggesting room for improved KG path filtering.
    *   **Limited Scope of KG Grounding:** The experiments use a relatively basic method for knowledge injection (simple prompt appending). More sophisticated KG-RAG methods exist and would provide a stronger evaluation.
    *   **Domain Coverage:** As acknowledged by the authors, the dataset relies primarily on Wikidata. Performance on domains requiring specialized knowledge (e.g., medical, legal) may be limited.  The dependence on a few datasets for much of the data (halueval, defan etc) reduces diversity.
    *   **Limited Languages:** While multilingual, the benchmark focuses on a small set of mostly high-resource European languages, which is good but not perfect, lacking typology.
    *   **Evaluation metrics**: Semantic similarity is not a great metric for factuality, though the best available without human evaluation.

*   **Potential Influence:**  MultiHal has the potential to become a valuable resource for researchers working on factuality evaluation, multilingual LLMs, and KG integration. It could drive innovation in hallucination mitigation techniques, leading to more trustworthy and reliable LLM systems.

*  **Lack of ablation studies**: The paper does not offer much ablation studies like varying the KG path length or testing if it can be improved by pre-training an LLM using the KG.

**Justification for Score:**

The score reflects the combination of the dataset's novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses. While MultiHal addresses an important gap in the literature, its reliance on LLM-as-a-judge, limited language scope, and relatively simple KG integration methods hold it back from achieving higher impact. It provides a good start, but requires extensions.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Legal Rule Induction: Towards Generalizable Principle Discovery from Analogous Judicial Precedents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14104v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Fan, Tianshi Zheng, Yiran Hu, Zheye Deng, Weiqi Wang, Baixuan Xu, Chunyang Li, Haoran Li, Weixing Shen, Yangqiu Song
- **Abstract**: Legal rules encompass not only codified statutes but also implicit adjudicatory principles derived from precedents that contain discretionary norms, social morality, and policy. While computational legal research has advanced in applying established rules to cases, inducing legal rules from judicial decisions remains understudied, constrained by limitations in model inference efficacy and symbolic reasoning capability. The advent of Large Language Models (LLMs) offers unprecedented opportunities for automating the extraction of such latent principles, yet progress is stymied by the absence of formal task definitions, benchmark datasets, and methodologies. To address this gap, we formalize Legal Rule Induction (LRI) as the task of deriving concise, generalizable doctrinal rules from sets of analogous precedents, distilling their shared preconditions, normative behaviors, and legal consequences. We introduce the first LRI benchmark, comprising 5,121 case sets (38,088 Chinese cases in total) for model tuning and 216 expert-annotated gold test sets. Experimental results reveal that: 1) State-of-the-art LLMs struggle with over-generalization and hallucination; 2) Training on our dataset markedly enhances LLMs capabilities in capturing nuanced rule patterns across similar cases.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Legal Rule Induction: Towards Generalizable Principle Discovery from Analogous Judicial Precedents":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of *legal rule induction* (LRI), which is the task of deriving generalizable legal principles from sets of analogous judicial precedents.  The authors argue that while existing computational legal research focuses primarily on applying established rules, the complementary inductive process of generating rules from cases is understudied. The paper formally defines LRI, proposes a three-element logical structure for legal rules (hypothetical condition, behavior pattern, and legal consequence), and introduces a new, large-scale Chinese legal case dataset called *LRI-Dataset*, comprising both an automatically curated training set (LRI-AUTO) and a meticulously annotated expert-labeled test set (LRI-GOLD).  The authors evaluate various Large Language Models (LLMs) on this task, including direct induction, chain-of-thought prompting, and a novel iterative induction-verification pipeline (SILVER). They also explore parameter-efficient fine-tuning (LoRA) on smaller LLMs using LRI-AUTO.  The results show that LLMs initially struggle with hallucination and overgeneralization, but that fine-tuning on LRI-AUTO significantly improves their ability to capture nuanced rule patterns.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in several aspects:
    *   **Formal Definition of LRI:**  Defining LRI as a distinct task with a clear objective and structural elements (hypothetical condition, behavior pattern, legal consequence) provides a much-needed formalization, setting the stage for future research.
    *   **LRI-Dataset:** The creation of the LRI-Dataset, especially the LRI-GOLD set with expert annotations, is a significant contribution. The scale and expert validation of the dataset address a critical gap in the field, enabling robust benchmarking and model development.  The focus on Chinese law, while limiting generalizability to common law systems without further work, offers a unique and valuable perspective, particularly given the structured nature of civil law judgments.
    *   **SILVER Pipeline:** The iterative induction-verification pipeline (SILVER) is a promising approach for refining rule generation, demonstrating the potential for automated improvement through multiple reasoning steps.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in computational legal reasoning by shifting focus towards *rule discovery* in addition to rule *application*. This inductive component is essential for bridging the gap between statutory and precedent-based reasoning and automating parts of legal work. The dataset and benchmarks provided will be instrumental for advancing research in this area.  Furthermore, the paper provides valuable insights into the challenges LLMs face in legal reasoning and offers evidence that task-specific training can substantially improve their performance. The findings about the strengths and weaknesses of different prompting methods (Direct Induction, CoT, SILVER) are also useful.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined Task:** The paper provides a clear and well-motivated formalization of the LRI task.
    *   **High-Quality Dataset:** The construction of the LRI-Dataset is thorough and rigorous, with a substantial expert-annotated test set.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper evaluates a diverse set of LLMs and techniques.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the methodology, experimental results, and analysis.
* **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Limited to Chinese Law:** The LRI-Dataset is based on Chinese legal cases. While this is a reasonable starting point due to the structured nature of civil law judgments, it limits the direct generalizability of the findings to other legal systems, especially common law traditions.
    *   **Reliance on LLMs for Data Curation:** The use of DeepSeek-R1 for automated data curation (e.g., case content structuring and rule extraction) introduces potential biases and inaccuracies. While the authors apply filtering, it's difficult to fully assess the impact of this reliance on LLMs on the quality of LRI-AUTO.
    *   **Limited Novelty of Individual Techniques:** The prompting strategies (Direct Induction, CoT) are well-established. The primary technical novelty is in the SILVER pipeline, but more analysis of the individual components of SILVER and their contributions would be beneficial.
    *   **LLM-as-a-Judge Concerns**: While the paper details a study to validate LLM-as-a-Judge's effectiveness and accuracy, this validation is specific to this kind of classification. There are known weaknesses with LLM-as-a-Judge and it's possible that there could be biases towards specific sentence structures, or even a lack of understanding of the rules themselves, leading to an inaccurate measure of effectiveness of rule extraction, and a flawed analysis if that is the basis.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of computational legal reasoning. The formal definition of LRI, the LRI-Dataset, and the initial evaluation results provide a solid foundation for future research in this area. The paper highlights the need for specialized training and methods for LLMs to effectively perform legal rule induction, and it encourages further investigation into iterative refinement approaches.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper.

* **7:** Indicates a strong contribution with significant novelty and impact, but with some limitations that need to be addressed in future research.

* **Rationale:** The paper introduces a novel task and a valuable dataset that has the potential to stimulate research in an important area of computational legal reasoning.  The limitations related to the dataset's focus on Chinese law and the reliance on LLMs for data curation prevent it from reaching a higher score at this time.  While individual techniques are not groundbreaking, the combination of formalization, dataset, and SILVER pipeline provides a solid foundation for future research and demonstrates its contribution is novel.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 0/10

### A Personalized Conversational Benchmark: Towards Simulating Personalized Conversations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14106v1)
- **Authors**: Li Li, Peilin Cai, Ryan A. Rossi, Franck Dernoncourt, Branislav Kveton, Junda Wu, Tong Yu, Linxin Song, Tiankai Yang, Yuehan Qin, Nesreen K. Ahmed, Samyadeep Basu, Subhojyoti Mukherjee, Ruiyi Zhang, Zhengmian Hu, Bo Ni, Yuxiao Zhou, Zichao Wang, Yue Huang, Yu Wang, Xiangliang Zhang, Philip S. Yu, Xiyang Hu, Yue Zhao
- **Abstract**: We present PersonaConvBench, a large-scale benchmark for evaluating personalized reasoning and generation in multi-turn conversations with large language models (LLMs). Unlike existing work that focuses on either personalization or conversational structure in isolation, PersonaConvBench integrates both, offering three core tasks: sentence classification, impact regression, and user-centric text generation across ten diverse Reddit-based domains. This design enables systematic analysis of how personalized conversational context shapes LLM outputs in realistic multi-user scenarios. We benchmark several commercial and open-source LLMs under a unified prompting setup and observe that incorporating personalized history yields substantial performance improvements, including a 198 percent relative gain over the best non-conversational baseline in sentiment classification. By releasing PersonaConvBench with evaluations and code, we aim to support research on LLMs that adapt to individual styles, track long-term context, and produce contextually rich, engaging responses.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PERSONACONVBENCH, a new benchmark designed to evaluate personalized reasoning and generation in multi-turn conversations for large language models (LLMs). Unlike existing benchmarks that focus on either personalization or conversational structure in isolation, PERSONACONVBENCH integrates both aspects. It offers three core tasks: sentence classification, impact regression, and user-centric text generation across 10 diverse Reddit-based domains. The authors benchmark several commercial and open-source LLMs and demonstrate that incorporating personalized conversational history significantly improves performance. The benchmark and code are released to facilitate further research in this area.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its integrated approach to personalization and conversational structure within a single benchmark. While personalization benchmarks and conversational benchmarks exist, the combination is relatively new and addresses a gap in evaluating LLMs' ability to simulate realistic multi-user conversational scenarios. This is a valuable step beyond single-turn personalization or user-agnostic conversational modeling.
*   **Significance:** The benchmark's significance is multi-faceted:
    *   **Addresses a real-world problem:** The paper correctly identifies the importance of personalization in applications such as social platforms, virtual assistants, and customer support.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** By providing three core tasks across diverse domains, the benchmark enables a comprehensive evaluation of LLMs' personalized conversational abilities.
    *   **Practical improvements demonstrated:** The experiments highlight the tangible performance gains achieved by incorporating personalized conversational history, reinforcing the benchmark's relevance.
    *   **Publicly available resource:** The release of the benchmark and code facilitates further research and development in the field.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clearly defined problem and tasks:** The paper clearly formulates the problem of personalized conversation generation and proposes well-defined tasks to address it.
    *   **Extensive experiments:** The benchmark includes a diverse set of domains and compares multiple LLMs.
    *   **Thorough analysis:** The authors provide a detailed analysis of the experimental results, highlighting the impact of personalized conversational history on performance.
    *   **Unified Framework**: The unified framework for the task structure and evaluation offers a rigorous and controlled testbed for models.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reddit-Specific Domains**: Drawing data solely from Reddit limits the benchmark's generalizability to other conversational contexts (e.g., customer service, professional collaborations).
    *   **Limited Tasks:** Although the paper covers 3 tasks, additional tasks that focus on reasoning, planning or goal-oriented interactions may strengthen the benchmark.
    *   **Zero-shot emphasis:** The primary evaluation focuses on zero-shot settings, which may not fully reflect the performance potential of LLMs when fine-tuned on personalized conversational data.
    *   **Limited Discussion:** The paper lacks a more extensive discussion of the ethical considerations (e.g. user data privacy, potential for misuse in malicious impersonation) relevant to personalized models.

*   **Potential Influence:** The benchmark has the potential to significantly influence research on LLMs by providing a standardized platform for evaluating personalized conversational abilities. It could drive the development of new techniques that enable LLMs to adapt to individual user styles and track long-term context, ultimately leading to more contextually rich and engaging responses.

**Overall:**

PERSONACONVBENCH represents a valuable contribution to the field of personalized conversational AI. The integrated approach, comprehensive evaluation, and publicly available resource are significant strengths. While there are limitations related to the data source and task variety, the benchmark's potential impact on driving research and development in this area justifies a high score.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### DiagnosisArena: Benchmarking Diagnostic Reasoning for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14107v1)
- **Authors**: Yakun Zhu, Zhongzhen Huang, Linjie Mu, Yutong Huang, Wei Nie, Shaoting Zhang, Pengfei Liu, Xiaofan Zhang
- **Abstract**: The emergence of groundbreaking large language models capable of performing complex reasoning tasks holds significant promise for addressing various scientific challenges, including those arising in complex clinical scenarios. To enable their safe and effective deployment in real-world healthcare settings, it is urgently necessary to benchmark the diagnostic capabilities of current models systematically. Given the limitations of existing medical benchmarks in evaluating advanced diagnostic reasoning, we present DiagnosisArena, a comprehensive and challenging benchmark designed to rigorously assess professional-level diagnostic competence. DiagnosisArena consists of 1,113 pairs of segmented patient cases and corresponding diagnoses, spanning 28 medical specialties, deriving from clinical case reports published in 10 top-tier medical journals. The benchmark is developed through a meticulous construction pipeline, involving multiple rounds of screening and review by both AI systems and human experts, with thorough checks conducted to prevent data leakage. Our study reveals that even the most advanced reasoning models, o3-mini, o1, and DeepSeek-R1, achieve only 45.82%, 31.09%, and 17.79% accuracy, respectively. This finding highlights a significant generalization bottleneck in current large language models when faced with clinical diagnostic reasoning challenges. Through DiagnosisArena, we aim to drive further advancements in AIs diagnostic reasoning capabilities, enabling more effective solutions for real-world clinical diagnostic challenges. We provide the benchmark and evaluation tools for further research and development https://github.com/SPIRAL-MED/DiagnosisArena.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the "DiagnosisArena: Benchmarking Diagnostic Reasoning for Large Language Models" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiagnosisArena, a new benchmark designed to assess the diagnostic reasoning abilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in complex clinical scenarios. It addresses limitations of existing medical benchmarks, which often rely on simplified knowledge recall rather than evaluating genuine diagnostic reasoning. DiagnosisArena consists of 1,113 patient cases extracted from top-tier medical journals, segmented and curated to represent realistic clinical complexities. The benchmark emphasizes professional-level diagnostic competence and employs a rigorous construction pipeline involving AI systems and human experts to ensure quality and prevent data leakage. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art LLMs on DiagnosisArena, revealing a significant generalization gap and highlighting the need for advancements in diagnostic reasoning capabilities for real-world clinical applications. They also provide the benchmark and evaluation tools for further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a novel and timely contribution to the field of medical AI by introducing a benchmark that focuses explicitly on *diagnostic reasoning* rather than simple knowledge recall. Existing benchmarks have been largely surpassed by current LLMs, making DiagnosisArena a much-needed resource for pushing the boundaries of AI in healthcare. The focus on cases from top-tier medical journals and the multi-stage curation process contribute to the realism and complexity of the benchmark.

*   **Significance:** DiagnosisArena holds significant potential for advancing AI in clinical diagnostics. By providing a challenging and realistic testbed, the benchmark can drive research efforts toward developing LLMs that can effectively analyze complex medical information and provide accurate diagnoses. This has important implications for improving patient care, reducing diagnostic errors, and assisting clinicians in decision-making.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Emphasis on Reasoning:** The key strength is the shift from simple knowledge assessment to a focus on the multifaceted process of diagnostic reasoning, mirroring real-world clinical challenges.
    *   **Realism:**  The use of cases from high-impact medical journals ensures that the benchmark is based on real clinical scenarios, increasing its relevance and practical value.
    *   **Rigorous Curation:** The multi-stage curation pipeline involving both AI systems and human experts increases the quality and reliability of the benchmark by preventing data leakage and addressing ambiguities in the cases.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors evaluate a variety of state-of-the-art LLMs and identify a significant gap in their performance, demonstrating the difficulty and value of the benchmark.
    *   **Publicly Available Resource:** The release of the benchmark and evaluation tools contributes to open research and collaboration in the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Potential for Bias in Curation:** Although rigorous, the curation process still introduces a level of subjectivity, as the selection of cases and diagnoses may be influenced by the experts' biases.
    *   **Limited Evaluation Metrics:** While using GPT-4o as a judge is reasonable, evaluation is still based on assessing if the models' answers are "identical", "relevant", or "irrelevant". This might be a too simplified approach that does not adequately reflect nuanced diagnostic decisions.

*   **Potential Influence:** DiagnosisArena has the potential to become a widely used benchmark in medical AI research, driving progress in diagnostic reasoning for LLMs. Its realism and complexity make it a valuable tool for evaluating and comparing different approaches and for identifying areas where further research is needed.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper's novelty and significance are undeniable given the current trajectory of LLM research and their application in healthcare. DiagnosisArena directly addresses a gap in existing benchmarks and contributes a resource that can stimulate further advancements in diagnostic reasoning capabilities. Although curation introduces a level of subjectivity and evaluation metric could be improved, the overall rigor and value of the benchmark are high.

### Invisible Entropy: Towards Safe and Efficient Low-Entropy LLM Watermarking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14112v1)
- **Authors**: Tianle Gu, Zongqi Wang, Kexin Huang, Yuanqi Yao, Xiangliang Zhang, Yujiu Yang, Xiuying Chen
- **Abstract**: Logit-based LLM watermarking traces and verifies AI-generated content by maintaining green and red token lists and increasing the likelihood of green tokens during generation. However, it fails in low-entropy scenarios, where predictable outputs make green token selection difficult without disrupting natural text flow. Existing approaches address this by assuming access to the original LLM to calculate entropy and selectively watermark high-entropy tokens. However, these methods face two major challenges: (1) high computational costs and detection delays due to reliance on the original LLM, and (2) potential risks of model leakage. To address these limitations, we propose Invisible Entropy (IE), a watermarking paradigm designed to enhance both safety and efficiency. Instead of relying on the original LLM, IE introduces a lightweight feature extractor and an entropy tagger to predict whether the entropy of the next token is high or low. Furthermore, based on theoretical analysis, we develop a threshold navigator that adaptively sets entropy thresholds. It identifies a threshold where the watermark ratio decreases as the green token count increases, enhancing the naturalness of the watermarked text and improving detection robustness. Experiments on HumanEval and MBPP datasets demonstrate that IE reduces parameter size by 99\% while achieving performance on par with state-of-the-art methods. Our work introduces a safe and efficient paradigm for low-entropy watermarking. https://github.com/Carol-gutianle/IE https://huggingface.co/datasets/Carol0110/IE-Tagger
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Invisible Entropy" (IE), a novel watermarking technique for large language models (LLMs) designed to address the challenges of existing methods in low-entropy scenarios, particularly in code generation.  Existing methods either disrupt natural text flow or require access to the original LLM, posing security and computational cost concerns. IE tackles these problems by using a lightweight feature extractor and entropy tagger to predict token entropy, eliminating the need for the original LLM during detection.  It further incorporates a "Threshold Navigator" to dynamically optimize entropy thresholds for improved watermark detectability and text naturalness.  Experiments on HumanEval and MBPP datasets demonstrate that IE achieves comparable performance to state-of-the-art methods with a 99% reduction in parameter size. The paper claims IE provides a safer, more efficient, and scalable solution for low-entropy watermarking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has several novel components. The primary innovation is the replacement of the original LLM with a lightweight feature extractor and entropy tagger for watermark detection. The idea of predicting entropy rather than directly calculating it is a sensible and potentially valuable contribution, especially considering security and cost constraints. The "Threshold Navigator" is also novel, providing a dynamic approach to entropy threshold optimization, which is an improvement over static thresholding.  However, the individual components of feature extraction and binary classification are not entirely novel in themselves; their *combination* for this specific application is where the novelty lies. This distinguishes it from directly regressing the entropy which they found to be difficult.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing critical issues with existing LLM watermarking techniques: security risks (model leakage) and computational cost.  Making watermarking more accessible and safer is an important step towards responsible AI.  The experimental results demonstrating comparable performance with significantly reduced parameter size are impactful and suggest practical benefits. The focus on low-entropy scenarios is also significant, as these are often neglected in watermarking research despite being common in domains like code generation. However, the practical impact will also depend on how well the system can adapt to different types of LLMs and tokenizers.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined and explained methodology.
    *   Comprehensive experiments on relevant datasets.
    *   Strong performance results with significant efficiency gains.
    *   Addresses important security and scalability concerns.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Robustness Testing:** The paper includes a brief test against paraphrasing attacks, but more comprehensive robustness evaluations would strengthen the findings.  How well does IE resist more sophisticated attacks designed to remove or disrupt the watermark?
    *   **Entropy Tagger Calibration**: App. C alludes to some slight decrease in calibration that future work could address. It could have been beneficial if they had included specific low-entropy tokens in the analysis.
    *   The approach may be heavily reliant on code-specific features, and the adaptability to other domains, while conceptually sound through unified embedding, might not be as straightforward. Future research should examine different domains and diverse data inputs to increase the approach's credibility.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field by promoting safer and more efficient watermarking methods for LLMs.  It can stimulate further research into entropy prediction techniques and dynamic threshold optimization.  The focus on low-entropy scenarios can inspire tailored watermarking solutions for specific applications. The reduced computational burden also opens doors to integration into resource-constrained environments.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a novel combination of techniques that addresses real-world limitations of existing LLM watermarking methods. The experimental results support the claims of improved safety, efficiency, and comparable performance. While there are some weaknesses in the robustness testing and scope of evaluation, the overall contribution is significant enough to warrant a high score.

### Self-Reasoning Language Models: Unfold Hidden Reasoning Chains with Few Reasoning Catalyst
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14116v1)
- **Authors**: Hongru Wang, Deng Cai, Wanjun Zhong, Shijue Huang, Jeff Z. Pan, Zeming Liu, Kam-Fai Wong
- **Abstract**: Inference-time scaling has attracted much attention which significantly enhance the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in complex reasoning tasks by increasing the length of Chain-of-Thought. These longer intermediate reasoning rationales embody various meta-reasoning skills in human cognition, such as reflection and decomposition, being difficult to create and acquire. In this work, we introduce \textit{Self-Reasoning Language Model} (SRLM), where the model itself can synthesize longer CoT data and iteratively improve performance through self-training. By incorporating a few demonstration examples (i.e., 1,000 samples) on how to unfold hidden reasoning chains from existing responses, which act as a reasoning catalyst, we demonstrate that SRLM not only enhances the model's initial performance but also ensures more stable and consistent improvements in subsequent iterations. Our proposed SRLM achieves an average absolute improvement of more than $+2.5$ points across five reasoning tasks: MMLU, GSM8K, ARC-C, HellaSwag, and BBH on two backbone models. Moreover, it brings more improvements with more times of sampling during inference, such as absolute $+7.89$ average improvement with $64$ sampling times, revealing the in-depth, diverse and creative reasoning paths in SRLM against the strong baseline.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'm ready to provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper based on the provided OCR.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Self-Reasoning Language Models" (SRLM), a framework designed to improve the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) through self-training. The core idea is to enable LLMs to synthesize longer and more complex Chain-of-Thought (CoT) data for self-improvement. The process involves: 1) Using a small set of "reasoning catalyst" data (demonstrations of how to extend reasoning chains) to fine-tune a base LLM. 2) Having the tuned LLM iteratively refine its own reasoning rationales by generating new CoT data, then selecting the best rationales using different selection strategies (length, on-policy, off-policy). 3) Combining the selected CoT data with the reasoning catalyst to train the model in the next iteration. The authors claim that SRLM can enhance the model's initial performance, ensure more stable improvements across iterations, and achieve better results than simply using GPT-4 generated CoT data for instruction tuning, especially in tasks needing complex reasoning. The method's effectiveness is tested on multiple benchmarks (MMLU, GSM8K, ARC-C, HellaSwag, BBH) using Llama3.1-8B and Mistral-7B models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to self-improvement in LLMs. The concept of using a *reasoning catalyst* is interesting as a way to guide the self-training process, rather than simply relying on self-generated data. The iterative refinement of CoT rationales using various selection strategies is also a worthwhile contribution. However, similar self-training frameworks are in the field, so it's not groundbreaking. It is incremental.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem: how to improve LLM reasoning without relying solely on human-annotated data or computationally expensive large models for data generation. If SRLM proves to be efficient and effective, it could have a significant impact on the practicality of improving LLMs in resource-constrained environments or for tasks where high-quality training data is scarce. The observed gains (average improvements across five benchmarks) are promising.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear and well-defined methodology:** The SRLM framework is clearly described, with the steps of reasoning catalyst acquisition, iterative reasoning expansion, and selection outlined in detail.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The paper provides experimental results across a diverse set of reasoning benchmarks, offering evidence for the effectiveness of SRLM. The comparison against strong baselines (Magpie, Reflection-tuning) is beneficial.
    *   **Ablation studies and analysis:** The authors conduct ablation studies (removing catalyst data) and analysis (different selectors, sampling times, and reasoning skill distributions) to provide insights into the behavior of SRLM.
    *   **Resource efficiency:** Emphasis on using smaller models (Llama3.1-8B, Mistral-7B) and a small set of reasoning catalyst data suggests that the method is practical and accessible.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited novelty in self-training:** Self-training and iterative refinement techniques are not new in machine learning or for LLMs, and the paper doesn't always clearly differentiate the novel aspects of SRLM from existing approaches. While the Reasoning Catalyst provides some nuance, more clarity could exist.
    *   **Reasoning catalyst creation process:** The method of creating the "reasoning catalyst" data (using GPT-4o) is not explained with sufficient detail and it introduces a dependency on a powerful model. While it's acknowledged that catalyst data is small in proportion, it is still influential. This raises questions about the scalability and independence of SRLM from high-resource models.
    *   **Ablations still show some improvement without the catalyst**. Figure 7 demonstrates that you can remove the catalyst and iterate, you still see some gain in performance suggesting that the gains are not soley attributable to the catalyst.
    *   **Generalizability concerns:** While the method is tested on multiple benchmarks, the results show variability across datasets and models. Some datasets even show plateaus or degradation through iteration. The effectiveness of SRLM might be sensitive to the specific characteristics of the task and the base LLM used.
    *   **Lack of theoretical analysis:** The paper lacks a theoretical explanation for why SRLM works. Why those specific types of Reasoning catalysts? What is the theory to justify the Selector method?
    *   **Qwen results degradation:** The Qwen-14B results indicate some issues. Since that model is already good, the catalyst and process might not be generalizable.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research on self-improving LLMs, especially by highlighting the importance of guiding the self-training process with curated demonstration data (the reasoning catalyst). Further research is needed to explore the optimal ways to create and utilize such catalyst data and to address the limitations of the current SRLM framework.

**Score:** 6/10

**Rationale:**

The SRLM paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM self-improvement. While the core concept of self-training isn't entirely novel, the introduction of the "reasoning catalyst" and the structured iterative refinement process demonstrate a worthwhile advancement. The extensive experiments provide solid empirical validation. However, the reliance on a powerful model (GPT-4o) for creating catalyst data, the somewhat limited generalizability across tasks and models, and the lack of a deeper theoretical analysis temper the overall impact. The lack of true groundbreaking innovation also counts against it. It's a solid, well-executed, and insightful study, but doesn't represent a major paradigm shift.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### MAS-KCL: Knowledge component graph structure learning with large language model-based agentic workflow
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14126v1)
- **Authors**: Yuan-Hao Jiang, Kezong Tang, Zi-Wei Chen, Yuang Wei, Tian-Yi Liu, Jiayi Wu
- **Abstract**: Knowledge components (KCs) are the fundamental units of knowledge in the field of education. A KC graph illustrates the relationships and dependencies between KCs. An accurate KC graph can assist educators in identifying the root causes of learners' poor performance on specific KCs, thereby enabling targeted instructional interventions. To achieve this, we have developed a KC graph structure learning algorithm, named MAS-KCL, which employs a multi-agent system driven by large language models for adaptive modification and optimization of the KC graph. Additionally, a bidirectional feedback mechanism is integrated into the algorithm, where AI agents leverage this mechanism to assess the value of edges within the KC graph and adjust the distribution of generation probabilities for different edges, thereby accelerating the efficiency of structure learning. We applied the proposed algorithm to 5 synthetic datasets and 4 real-world educational datasets, and experimental results validate its effectiveness in learning path recognition. By accurately identifying learners' learning paths, teachers are able to design more comprehensive learning plans, enabling learners to achieve their educational goals more effectively, thus promoting the sustainable development of education.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper based on the provided text.

**Summary**

The paper "MAS-KCL: Knowledge Component Graph Structure Learning with Large Language Model-based Agentic Workflow" introduces a novel algorithm (MAS-KCL) for learning the structure of knowledge component (KC) graphs. KC graphs represent the relationships and dependencies between fundamental units of knowledge in a specific domain, and accurate KC graphs can assist educators in identifying learning gaps and providing targeted instruction. MAS-KCL employs a multi-agent system driven by large language models (LLMs) to adaptively modify and optimize the KC graph structure.  It also uses a bidirectional feedback mechanism to assess the value of edges and adjust generation probabilities. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of MAS-KCL on both synthetic and real-world educational datasets, claiming it improves learning path recognition and helps teachers design comprehensive learning plans. The inclusion of an LLM-based multi-agent system and the use of a bidirectional feedback loop are highlighted as key contributions.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of a multi-agent system, driven by LLMs, with a bidirectional feedback mechanism for KC graph structure learning. While other approaches have explored KC graph learning using Bayesian Networks, GNNs, and reinforcement learning, the LLM-driven multi-agent approach with feedback appears to be a unique combination. This allows for a more adaptive and potentially interpretable learning process. However, some existing works use similar LLM-driven techniques for graph learning and personalized learning plans that need further comparison.
*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this research is significant. By improving the accuracy of KC graphs, educators can gain a better understanding of students' learning paths and tailor instruction more effectively. This could lead to improved learning outcomes and more sustainable educational development, as the authors suggest. The use of LLMs also has the potential to make the process more efficient and scalable.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The use of both synthetic and real-world datasets provides a comprehensive evaluation of the algorithm's performance.
    *   The ablation studies demonstrate the importance of the multi-agent system and each individual agent to the overall performance.
    *   The paper provides a clear and detailed description of the MAS-KCL algorithm.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper could benefit from a more thorough comparison to existing methods. While the authors mention some related work, a direct comparison of performance against state-of-the-art graph learning algorithms on the same datasets would strengthen the claims.
    *   The reliance on LLMs introduces a potential dependence on the quality and availability of these models. Further investigation into the robustness of MAS-KCL with different LLMs is necessary. Also, a clear explanation of prompt engineering techniques is needed.
    *   The paper could further explore the interpretability of the learned KC graphs. While the authors claim the approach is interpretable, more detail on how educators can use the learned graphs to inform their instruction would be valuable. Further examples are also needed to support claims.
    *   The algorithm's complexity and computational cost should be explicitly addressed, especially considering the use of LLMs.

*   **Potential Influence:** The MAS-KCL algorithm has the potential to influence the field of intelligent tutoring systems and educational data mining. The LLM-driven multi-agent approach could inspire new methods for learning and representing knowledge structures in educational contexts.
*   **Justification of the Score:** The paper presents a novel and promising approach to KC graph structure learning. However, the lack of a more rigorous comparison to existing methods and potential limitations related to the reliance on LLMs temper the overall impact.

### Texts or Images? A Fine-grained Analysis on the Effectiveness of Input Representations and Models for Table Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14131v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Zhou, Mohsen Mesgar, Heike Adel, Annemarie Friedrich
- **Abstract**: In table question answering (TQA), tables are encoded as either texts or images. Prior work suggests that passing images of tables to multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) performs comparably to or even better than using textual input with large language models (LLMs). However, the lack of controlled setups limits fine-grained distinctions between these approaches. In this paper, we conduct the first controlled study on the effectiveness of several combinations of table representations and models from two perspectives: question complexity and table size. We build a new benchmark based on existing TQA datasets. In a systematic analysis of seven pairs of MLLMs and LLMs, we find that the best combination of table representation and model varies across setups. We propose FRES, a method selecting table representations dynamically, and observe a 10% average performance improvement compared to using both representations indiscriminately.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Texts or Images? A Fine-grained Analysis on the Effectiveness of Input Representations and Models for Table Question Answering":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of different input representations (text vs. image) and models (LLMs vs. MLLMs) for table question answering (TQA).  The authors argue that existing work lacks controlled studies to understand the nuances of these approaches. To address this, they create a new TQA benchmark derived from six existing datasets, carefully controlling for question complexity (retrieval vs. reasoning) and table size (small vs. big). They evaluate seven open-weight MLLMs and their corresponding LLM decoders in a zero-shot setting.  Key findings include that the best model-representation combination varies depending on the task and table characteristics, and that large models generally benefit from image input, while smaller models are more sensitive to the interplay between representation and task complexity. They propose FRES, a feature-based table representation selection method, that dynamically chooses between text and image representations for small models, achieving performance improvements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic and controlled approach to studying input representation for TQA. While previous works have compared text and image inputs, they often haven't controlled for confounding variables like question complexity and table size. The creation of the new benchmark and the detailed analysis using open-weight models contributes incremental but valuable insights. The FRES method itself, while relatively simple, is a practical application of the findings. The paper presents a much more controlled setup than most previous work.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is moderate. It provides practical guidance for selecting appropriate input representations and models for TQA, particularly in resource-constrained settings where smaller models are used. The identification of specific scenarios where image input is beneficial vs. when text input is preferred is valuable for practitioners. The controlled experiments help to disentangle factors that contribute to performance, leading to a more nuanced understanding of the problem. The release of the new benchmark contributes a valuable asset to the community, encouraging future studies of open-weight models and controlled experimental setups.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The controlled experimental design is a major strength.
    *   The use of open-weight models increases reproducibility and accessibility.
    *   The creation of the new benchmark adds value to the research community.
    *   The feature-based approach contributes a practical solution.
    *   Carefully accounts for data contamination, which is a big deal in the current research landscape.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty of the individual components is limited (e.g., feature-based representation selection is not entirely new).
    *   The gains from FRES, while significant, aren't groundbreaking. 10% improvement isn't all that revolutionary, though it's still a useful result.
    *   The benchmark, while controlled, is still derived from existing datasets, which might limit its ability to uncover truly novel insights.
    * The paper does not include discussion of uncertainty estimation from LLMs, especially when selecting which questions are reasoning-based.
    * The paper only focuses on a zero-shot setting. The results may differ when models are fine-tuned for TQA.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in TQA by promoting more controlled experiments and the use of open-weight models. The benchmark could become a standard tool for evaluating new approaches. The FRES method might inspire more sophisticated approaches to dynamic representation selection.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper offers a valuable and systematic analysis of input representation effectiveness for TQA. Its controlled experiments, open-weight focus, and practical FRES method represent a solid contribution to the field. While the novelty of individual components is incremental, the combination of these elements in a rigorous and well-executed study justifies a score of 7. The findings have practical implications for TQA system design and the new benchmark will aid future research in this area. The weaknesses regarding modest gains, a limited benchmark and the lack of fine-tuning consideration prevent a higher score. Also, the limitations don't undermine the significance of the work, but they limit its overall impact. The lack of novelty of individual components further constrains the overall assessment.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Hunyuan-Game: Industrial-grade Intelligent Game Creation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14135v1)
- **Authors**: Ruihuang Li, Caijin Zhou, Shoujian Zheng, Jianxiang Lu, Jiabin Huang, Comi Chen, Junshu Tang, Guangzheng Xu, Jiale Tao, Hongmei Wang, Donghao Li, Wenqing Yu, Senbo Wang, Zhimin Li, Yetshuan Shi, Haoyu Yang, Yukun Wang, Wenxun Dai, Jiaqi Li, Linqing Wang, Qixun Wang, Zhiyong Xu, Yingfang Zhang, Jiangfeng Xiong, Weijie Kong, Chao Zhang, Hongxin Zhang, Qiaoling Zheng, Weiting Guo, Xinchi Deng, Yixuan Li, Renjia Wei, Yulin Jian, Duojun Huang, Xuhua Ren, Sihuan Lin, Yifu Sun, Yuan Zhou, Joey Wang, Qin Lin, Jingmiao Yu, Jihong Zhang, Caesar Zhong, Di Wang, Yuhong Liu, Linus, Jie Jiang, Longhuang Wu, Shuai Shao, Qinglin Lu
- **Abstract**: Intelligent game creation represents a transformative advancement in game development, utilizing generative artificial intelligence to dynamically generate and enhance game content. Despite notable progress in generative models, the comprehensive synthesis of high-quality game assets, including both images and videos, remains a challenging frontier. To create high-fidelity game content that simultaneously aligns with player preferences and significantly boosts designer efficiency, we present Hunyuan-Game, an innovative project designed to revolutionize intelligent game production. Hunyuan-Game encompasses two primary branches: image generation and video generation. The image generation component is built upon a vast dataset comprising billions of game images, leading to the development of a group of customized image generation models tailored for game scenarios: (1) General Text-to-Image Generation. (2) Game Visual Effects Generation, involving text-to-effect and reference image-based game visual effect generation. (3) Transparent Image Generation for characters, scenes, and game visual effects. (4) Game Character Generation based on sketches, black-and-white images, and white models. The video generation component is built upon a comprehensive dataset of millions of game and anime videos, leading to the development of five core algorithmic models, each targeting critical pain points in game development and having robust adaptation to diverse game video scenarios: (1) Image-to-Video Generation. (2) 360 A/T Pose Avatar Video Synthesis. (3) Dynamic Illustration Generation. (4) Generative Video Super-Resolution. (5) Interactive Game Video Generation. These image and video generation models not only exhibit high-level aesthetic expression but also deeply integrate domain-specific knowledge, establishing a systematic understanding of diverse game and anime art styles.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Hunyuan-Game: Intelligent Game Creation with Generative AI":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Hunyuan-Game, a comprehensive suite of generative AI models designed to revolutionize game asset creation. It comprises four image generation models (text-to-image, game visual effects, transparent image generation, game character generation) and five video generation models (image-to-video, 360° A/T pose avatar video synthesis, dynamic illustration generation, video super-resolution, interactive game video generation). The models are trained on billions of game images and millions of game/anime videos.  A key focus is on domain-specific knowledge integration and aesthetic control, aiming to overcome limitations of general-purpose generative models in the gaming context.  The paper claims state-of-the-art performance in visual fidelity and motion naturalness compared to models like Midjourney, Kling, and Wan. The goal is to accelerate game development, foster community-driven innovation, and enable broader AI applications within the gaming industry.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  While the *combination* of all these modules into a single framework is a significant undertaking, the individual components are not groundbreaking *per se*.  Many of the underlying techniques, such as diffusion transformers, LoRA fine-tuning, and image/video editing approaches, are established. The *novelty* primarily lies in the *application* of these techniques *specifically* to the game asset creation domain and the engineering effort in building the large-scale datasets and customized training pipelines.  The claim of "industry first" for some modules (like text-to-game effects generation) needs to be carefully examined. Are these genuinely novel applications or slight variations of existing ideas? What specific technical innovations enable these capabilities that differentiate them significantly?

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential impact on game development workflows. If Hunyuan-Game genuinely delivers on its promise of high-quality, controllable, and efficient asset generation, it could substantially reduce development costs and accelerate the creative process.  However, the paper's significance is contingent upon the demonstrated practical utility and adoption by game developers. The lack of in-depth *user studies* or *case studies* showcasing the quantifiable benefits in real-world game development scenarios is a significant weakness.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The breadth of the framework, encompassing both image and video generation across multiple asset types, is a considerable achievement.
    *   **Domain Focus:** The emphasis on domain-specific knowledge and aesthetic standards is crucial for generating assets that meet the specific requirements of game development.
    *   **Large-Scale Data:** The use of massive datasets tailored for gaming and anime is likely a key enabler of the reported performance.
    *   **Potential for Impact:** The potential to accelerate game development and empower creators is substantial.

*   **Weaknesses:**

The metrics used (T-I Alignment, Game Concept, Subject Modeling, Aesthetics, Overall) are somewhat subjective and lack rigorous definition. The lack of comparisons on *standard* datasets or well-established quantitative metrics common in the generative AI literature is a significant drawback. There is a need to compare the approach quantitatively with other state-of-the-art methods, using standardized metrics.
    *   **Subjective Comparisons:** The qualitative comparisons, while visually appealing, are subjective. A more rigorous evaluation would involve blind user studies where game developers compare assets generated by Hunyuan-Game and other methods.
    *   **Lack of Real-World Application Evidence:** The paper lacks concrete evidence of the system's practical utility in actual game development projects. Detailed case studies or user feedback would significantly strengthen the claims of impact.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the system is as effective as claimed, it could influence game development pipelines, foster new creative workflows, and potentially democratize access to high-quality game asset creation. However, the long-term influence depends on the adoption by the gaming industry and the availability of the tools to the broader community.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:**  The paper presents an ambitious project targeting a significant challenge in the gaming industry.  It combines known techniques in a novel way and builds large datasets, but the lack of detailed technical information, robust quantitative comparisons, and real-world application evidence limits its immediate impact.

**Justification:**

A score of 6 reflects the potential impact of this work balanced against its current limitations. The project demonstrates significant engineering effort and a clear understanding of the needs of the gaming industry.  However, the lack of significant *technical* novelty in the core algorithms, the limited and subjective evaluation, and the absence of real-world validation prevent a higher score. The paper has the potential to be highly influential, but its impact remains to be seen pending further detailed publications, more rigorous evaluations, and demonstrated adoption within the game development community. ### FlowQ: Energy-Guided Flow Policies for Offline Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14139v1)
- **Authors**: Marvin Alles, Nutan Chen, Patrick van der Smagt, Botond Cseke
- **Abstract**: The use of guidance to steer sampling toward desired outcomes has been widely explored within diffusion models, especially in applications such as image and trajectory generation. However, incorporating guidance during training remains relatively underexplored. In this work, we introduce energy-guided flow matching, a novel approach that enhances the training of flow models and eliminates the need for guidance at inference time. We learn a conditional velocity field corresponding to the flow policy by approximating an energy-guided probability path as a Gaussian path. Learning guided trajectories is appealing for tasks where the target distribution is defined by a combination of data and an energy function, as in reinforcement learning. Diffusion-based policies have recently attracted attention for their expressive power and ability to capture multi-modal action distributions. Typically, these policies are optimized using weighted objectives or by back-propagating gradients through actions sampled by the policy. As an alternative, we propose FlowQ, an offline reinforcement learning algorithm based on energy-guided flow matching. Our method achieves competitive performance while the policy training time is constant in the number of flow sampling steps.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "FlowQ: Energy-Guided Flow Policies for Offline Reinforcement Learning" introduces a novel offline reinforcement learning (RL) algorithm. FlowQ leverages energy-guided flow matching to learn a policy that approximates the optimal policy from a static dataset. Instead of directly learning a transformation from a source distribution to the data distribution (as in standard flow matching), FlowQ learns a transformation to an energy-weighted distribution, guided by a Q-function (representing an energy function). The key advantage is that FlowQ eliminates the need for backpropagation through actions sampled from the policy during training, leading to a more efficient and scalable approach, particularly when using a large number of flow sampling steps. The authors demonstrate competitive performance on the D4RL benchmark compared to other offline RL methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of energy-guided flow matching is a novel contribution. While the connection between diffusion models and RL has been explored, the specific formulation of learning a flow to an energy-weighted distribution *during training*, rather than using guidance *at inference time*, is a distinguishing feature. Also, using a *linear Taylor expansion to approximate the energy* makes the method efficient. The method also makes use of flow matching which is newer than diffusion models.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of FlowQ on the field of offline RL stems from its scalability. Many existing diffusion-based policies for offline RL require costly backpropagation through actions sampled from the model during training, which limits their applicability to scenarios with complex action spaces or requiring high-fidelity policies (i.e., many diffusion steps). FlowQ mitigates this limitation, potentially enabling the training of more expressive and effective policies.  This is significant, as it addresses a practical bottleneck in deploying diffusion-based policies in RL.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Efficiency:** The paper clearly highlights the improved computational efficiency compared to methods that require backpropagation through sampled actions. This is a substantial advantage.
    *   **Competitive Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate competitive performance on the D4RL benchmark, showing that FlowQ is a viable alternative to existing offline RL algorithms.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper provides a well-structured and technically sound presentation of the proposed method.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Approximation Errors:** The use of the linear Taylor expansion to approximate the energy function introduces an approximation that could limit performance, especially for complex energy landscapes.  The approximation E(xt) ≈ λ(t)E(xt) in Section 4.1 is also a limitation as highlighted in Section 5.4, and its impact could be further explored.
    *   **Limited Ablation Study:** The ablation study is somewhat limited. While it examines the impact of the time-dependent scaling function h(t), it could benefit from a more thorough investigation of the sensitivity of the algorithm to the choice of hyperparameters, especially the scaling parameter λ, and the impact of using other approximations of the energy function.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While novel, the improvements over existing methods on some D4RL tasks are not overwhelmingly large.

*   **Potential Influence:** FlowQ has the potential to influence future research in offline RL by encouraging the development of more scalable diffusion-based policies. Its focus on eliminating backpropagation through actions could inspire new approaches that further reduce the computational burden of training expressive policies in the offline setting. It might also lead to a deeper investigation of better energy function approximations and the use of Flow Matching in place of Diffusion models.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel method, FlowQ, which makes a significant contribution to the field of offline RL by tackling the issue of scalability in diffusion-based policy learning. The removal of the need to backpropagate through actions sampled from the flow model addresses a practical bottleneck. It is not a massive breakthrough that completely overthrows the landscape of offline RL, since the performance gain over existing methods is somewhat incremental on some D4RL tasks. This is partially offset by weaknesses in the ablation study. The experiments, while thorough, do not overwhelmingly establish superiority across all D4RL tasks.

Score: 7

The score of 7 reflects the paper's novel contribution to energy-guided flow matching for efficient offline RL, its competitive performance on the D4RL benchmark, and its clear presentation, tempered by concerns regarding approximation errors, limited ablation studies, and the magnitude of performance gains.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### RL of Thoughts: Navigating LLM Reasoning with Inference-time Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14140v1)
- **Authors**: Qianyue Hao, Sibo Li, Jian Yuan, Yong Li
- **Abstract**: Despite rapid advancements in large language models (LLMs), the token-level autoregressive nature constrains their complex reasoning capabilities. To enhance LLM reasoning, inference-time techniques, including Chain/Tree/Graph-of-Thought(s), successfully improve the performance, as they are fairly cost-effective by guiding reasoning through sophisticated logical structures without modifying LLMs' parameters. However, these manually predefined, task-agnostic frameworks are applied uniformly across diverse tasks, lacking adaptability. To improve this, we propose RL-of-Thoughts (RLoT), where we train a lightweight navigator model with reinforcement learning (RL) to adaptively enhance LLM reasoning at inference time. Specifically, we design five basic logic blocks from the perspective of human cognition. During the reasoning process, the trained RL navigator dynamically selects the suitable logic blocks and combines them into task-specific logical structures according to problem characteristics. Experiments across multiple reasoning benchmarks (AIME, MATH, GPQA, etc.) with multiple LLMs (GPT, Llama, Qwen, and DeepSeek) illustrate that RLoT outperforms established inference-time techniques by up to 13.4%. Remarkably, with less than 3K parameters, our RL navigator is able to make sub-10B LLMs comparable to 100B-scale counterparts. Moreover, the RL navigator demonstrates strong transferability: a model trained on one specific LLM-task pair can effectively generalize to unseen LLMs and tasks. Our code is open-source at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/RL-LLM-Reasoning-1A30 for reproducibility.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "RL of Thoughts: Navigating LLM Reasoning with Inference-time Reinforcement Learning" proposes RLoT, a novel inference-time technique to enhance the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). RLoT trains a small, lightweight "navigator" model using reinforcement learning (RL) to dynamically select and combine basic logic blocks (inspired by human cognition) during the LLM's reasoning process. This allows for the adaptive construction of task-specific logical structures, rather than relying on fixed, task-agnostic approaches like Chain-of-Thought (CoT). Experiments across various reasoning benchmarks and LLMs demonstrate that RLoT outperforms existing inference-time techniques, even enabling smaller LLMs to achieve performance comparable to much larger models. The navigator model also exhibits strong transferability across different LLMs and tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant advance in inference-time LLM reasoning. The core idea of using RL to dynamically construct reasoning strategies is novel and addresses a key limitation of existing methods, which rely on manually designed, inflexible logical structures. While RL has been applied to LLMs in the past (often for fine-tuning), this paper uniquely leverages it at inference time, offering a more adaptable and efficient approach. The design of the logic blocks themselves, while inspired by cognitive processes, isn't necessarily groundbreaking on its own, but their integration within the RL framework is where the novelty lies.
