It also opens up new avenues for research in areas such as automatic curriculum learning, where the difficulty of CoT examples can be adjusted by introducing or removing Thought Leaps.

* **Concerns & Mitigations:** I do see potential concerns about the generalizability of the ScaleQM+ based bridge model, since many of the improvements come from bridging a relatively consistent format of reasoning step. However, the authors could mitigate this concern by further analysis on the types of bridging steps being implemented, to better demonstrate structural understanding rather than memorization of ScaleQM+.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel problem formulation with a solid solution and strong empirical evidence. The significance of the work lies in its potential to improve the robustness and generalization capabilities of LLMs on reasoning tasks. While there are some limitations, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses, and the paper is likely to have a positive impact on the field. Given that the techniques are somewhat standard, the dependence on the CoT-Bridge and ScaleQM+ formats limit the absolute generality of the findings, and that there is not human validation, I cannot give the paper a rating higher than 7/10, despite the interesting premise and strong results.

**Score: 7/10**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Grouping First, Attending Smartly: Training-Free Acceleration for Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14687v1)
- **Authors**: Sucheng Ren, Qihang Yu, Ju He, Alan Yuille, Liang-Chieh Chen
- **Abstract**: Diffusion-based Transformers have demonstrated impressive generative capabilities, but their high computational costs hinder practical deployment, for example, generating an $8192\times 8192$ image can take over an hour on an A100 GPU. In this work, we propose GRAT (\textbf{GR}ouping first, \textbf{AT}tending smartly), a training-free attention acceleration strategy for fast image and video generation without compromising output quality. The key insight is to exploit the inherent sparsity in learned attention maps (which tend to be locally focused) in pretrained Diffusion Transformers and leverage better GPU parallelism. Specifically, GRAT first partitions contiguous tokens into non-overlapping groups, aligning both with GPU execution patterns and the local attention structures learned in pretrained generative Transformers. It then accelerates attention by having all query tokens within the same group share a common set of attendable key and value tokens. These key and value tokens are further restricted to structured regions, such as surrounding blocks or criss-cross regions, significantly reducing computational overhead (e.g., attaining a \textbf{35.8$\times$} speedup over full attention when generating $8192\times 8192$ images) while preserving essential attention patterns and long-range context. We validate GRAT on pretrained Flux and HunyuanVideo for image and video generation, respectively. In both cases, GRAT achieves substantially faster inference without any fine-tuning, while maintaining the performance of full attention. We hope GRAT will inspire future research on accelerating Diffusion Transformers for scalable visual generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Grouping First, Attending Smartly: Training-Free Acceleration for Diffusion Transformers":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GRAT (Grouping first, Attending smartly), a novel, training-free acceleration strategy for Diffusion Transformers. GRAT tackles the computational bottleneck of self-attention by first grouping contiguous tokens in the feature map and then restricting attention to structured regions (surrounding blocks or criss-cross patterns). This approach aims to maintain visual quality and long-range context while significantly reducing computational overhead and improving GPU parallelism. The authors validate their method on pre-trained Flux and HunyuanVideo models, demonstrating substantial inference speedups without fine-tuning or significant performance degradation. The core idea is to exploit the inherent sparsity and locality observed in attention maps of pre-trained Diffusion Transformers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of GRAT, grouping tokens and attending to structured regions, presents a reasonable advancement in attention mechanism optimization for Diffusion Transformers. While locality in attention has been previously explored, the specific combination of grouping with structured attention and the demonstrated effectiveness without retraining does offer novelty. The two variants, GRAT-B and GRAT-X, provide different trade-offs between speed and receptive field, further adding to the novelty. However, the individual components (grouping, local attention, criss-cross attention) are not entirely new, but rather combined in a novel way within the Diffusion Transformer context.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of GRAT lies in enabling faster inference for Diffusion Transformers, particularly for high-resolution image and video generation. Reducing the computational cost makes these models more accessible and practical for real-world applications. The training-free aspect is particularly significant, as it allows for easy integration with pre-trained models. However, the paper primarily focuses on demonstrating speedups and visual quality with existing models. While significant, this does not directly translate to new capabilities or a paradigm shift in generative modeling.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-free:** The fact that GRAT requires no fine-tuning is a substantial advantage, enabling plug-and-play deployment with existing pre-trained models.
    *   **Significant Speedup:**  The reported speedups (up to 35.8x for image generation and 15.8x for video generation) are considerable and represent a practical improvement.
    *   **Maintained Visual Quality:**  The authors demonstrate that GRAT can achieve significant speedups without significant degradation in visual quality, a crucial factor for generative models. The quantitative results support this claim, with minimal drops in FID and other metrics.
    *   **Strong experimental validation:** The paper demonstrates broad and well-executed experiments in two different tasks, and validates performance in different image size settings.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written, explains the concepts clearly, and provides adequate visualizations and experimental details.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Hand-designed patterns:** The grouping and attention patterns are manually designed, which may limit adaptability to different architectures or datasets.  The paper does not explore how to learn these patterns adaptively, a potential area for future improvement.
    *   **Limited theoretical analysis:** While the paper provides empirical evidence of the effectiveness of GRAT, there is a limited theoretical analysis of why this approach works and how it relates to the underlying properties of Diffusion Transformers.  Some justification on what range dependencies are important for a Diffusion Transformer is needed.
    *   **Limited ablation study:** The ablation study primarily focuses on group size and block size parameters.  A more comprehensive ablation study exploring the impact of different group shapes or attention patterns could provide further insights.

*   **Potential Influence:** GRAT can influence future research on efficient attention mechanisms for Diffusion Transformers and other generative models. The idea of structured sparse attention may inspire new approaches that learn adaptive attention patterns or leverage hardware-specific optimizations.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The score acknowledges the practical benefits of GRAT in accelerating Diffusion Transformer inference without compromising quality. However, the lack of theoretical depth, the manually designed attention patterns, and the limited ablation study prevent a higher score. The idea of grouping and attending smartly exploits the visual structure in generative models, but is somewhat of an incremental contribution over previous methods that exploit spatial locality in attention.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
